Prior MoE approaches in multimodal tasks have typically relied on purely neural routing mechanisms. The use of *semantic roles as a prior* into the routing strategy is a promising contribution.

    *   **Expert Specialization with TableMoE-Align:** The explicit separation of table understanding into HTML layout, JSON triples and code abstraction through dedicated experts is a strong architectural novelty

    *   **WildStruct Benchmarks:** Recognizing and formally defining the challenges posed by "WildStruct" tables is valuable. The curated benchmarks fill a gap in the evaluation of table understanding models.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in multimodal learning: understanding and reasoning over complex, real-world tables. Tables are a fundamental medium for structured data communication, and improving their understanding has broad implications. The introduced benchmarks and the TableMoE architecture push the field toward more robust and generalizable solutions. By isolating and addressing the WildStruct challenge, the paper points out vital architectural changes for improved performance in realistic conditions.
    *   The impact of this work is significant. The field relies heavily on large language models (LLMs), which lack the structured decomposition that tables require. The interpretable reasoning, function-aware expert activation, and the ability to maintain robust performance are valuable attributes for real-world applications.
    *   The results are quantitatively significant, with gains of up to 9.2% over strong baselines.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The combination of neuro-symbolic reasoning with MoE is a powerful architectural choice.
    *   The release of TableMoE-Align and the WildStruct benchmarks provides valuable resources for the research community.
    *   The thorough experimental evaluation, including ablation studies and qualitative analyses, provides strong evidence for the effectiveness of TableMoE's components.
    *   The interpretable routing mechanism and expert specialization enhance understanding of the model's reasoning process.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper demonstrates improved robustness, the method still likely relies on the pretraining of the individual components. While the authors address this, a potential direction to explore could be how TableMoE can learn to be more robust to degraded tables without any prior training data

    *   Computational Cost:  Mixture-of-expert architectures are known to be computationally expensive, especially at scale. Although co-upcycled, more details regarding the increase in parameters/computational cost relative to standard LLMs and how it impacts training and inference would be helpful.

    *   Limited Domain Diversity in Benchmarks: Although the authors incorporated other types of documents, WMM series were primarily built for finance datasets. A more diverse set of WildStruct datasets, spanning broader areas, is needed to ensure TableMoE's broad applicability.

    *   There are instances of a disconnect in evaluation between the WildStruct benchmark's goal (addressing noise and corruption) and the reliance on pretraining in realistic real-world data.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper is likely to influence future research on multimodal table understanding by highlighting the importance of structured reasoning and robustness to real-world degradation.
    *   The WildStruct benchmarks will serve as a valuable resource for evaluating and comparing future models.
    *   The neuro-symbolic MoE architecture could inspire new approaches to other complex multimodal tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 8. The paper makes substantial contributions to the field of multimodal table understanding by introducing a novel neuro-symbolic MoE architecture, releasing valuable datasets and benchmarks, and demonstrating significant performance improvements. The architecture provides an efficient way of dealing with structured and unstructured data.

While computationally intensive, the technique provides substantial improvement to LLMs which have become the dominant method of multimodal representation. The work takes a significant step towards more robust and generalizable table understanding systems.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### Scalable Bayesian Low-Rank Adaptation of Large Language Models via Stochastic Variational Subspace Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21408v1)
- **Authors**: Colin Samplawski, Adam D. Cobb, Manoj Acharya, Ramneet Kaur, Susmit Jha
- **Abstract**: Despite their widespread use, large language models (LLMs) are known to hallucinate incorrect information and be poorly calibrated. This makes the uncertainty quantification of these models of critical importance, especially in high-stakes domains, such as autonomy and healthcare. Prior work has made Bayesian deep learning-based approaches to this problem more tractable by performing inference over the low-rank adaptation (LoRA) parameters of a fine-tuned model. While effective, these approaches struggle to scale to larger LLMs due to requiring further additional parameters compared to LoRA. In this work we present $\textbf{Scala}$ble $\textbf{B}$ayesian $\textbf{L}$ow-Rank Adaptation via Stochastic Variational Subspace Inference (ScalaBL). We perform Bayesian inference in an $r$-dimensional subspace, for LoRA rank $r$. By repurposing the LoRA parameters as projection matrices, we are able to map samples from this subspace into the full weight space of the LLM. This allows us to learn all the parameters of our approach using stochastic variational inference. Despite the low dimensionality of our subspace, we are able to achieve competitive performance with state-of-the-art approaches while only requiring ${\sim}1000$ additional parameters. Furthermore, it allows us to scale up to the largest Bayesian LLM to date, with four times as a many base parameters as prior work.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ScalaBL, a method for scalable Bayesian low-rank adaptation of large language models (LLMs) using stochastic variational subspace inference. ScalaBL performs Bayesian inference in a low-dimensional subspace of the LoRA parameters, repurposing the LoRA parameters as projection matrices. This approach allows for learning with stochastic variational inference using only a small number of additional parameters, scaling up to larger LLMs while maintaining competitive performance in uncertainty quantification on commonsense reasoning benchmarks.  A key claim is that ScalaBL requires significantly fewer additional parameters compared to prior work like BLoB and scales effectively to very large models (32B parameters).

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the combination of Bayesian subspace inference with LoRA, specifically in the way the LoRA parameters are reinterpreted as projection matrices. While Bayesian subspace inference and LoRA are individually established techniques, the paper demonstrates an ingenious way to combine them, leading to a highly parameter-efficient Bayesian fine-tuning method. This is a significant step forward in making Bayesian methods practical for LLMs.  The method avoids learning a separate projection matrix as in previous subspace approaches.

* **Significance:** The significance of this paper is threefold:
    1.  **Scalability:**  It demonstrably scales Bayesian inference to larger LLMs than previously achieved, which is crucial for applying these techniques in real-world applications where model size often correlates with performance.
    2.  **Parameter Efficiency:** It presents a method that is significantly more parameter-efficient than BLoB. This efficiency is vital in resource-constrained environments and lowers the barrier to entry for researchers wanting to apply Bayesian methods.
    3.  **Competitive Performance:** It maintains competitive or even superior performance compared to state-of-the-art approaches in uncertainty quantification, while using fewer parameters. This shows that parameter efficiency doesn't necessarily come at the cost of accuracy.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong empirical evaluation:** The paper provides extensive experimental results across multiple datasets and model sizes, comparing ScalaBL with various baselines.
    *   **Clear problem definition and solution:** The problem of scaling Bayesian inference to large LLMs is well-defined, and the paper presents a clear and concise solution.
    *   **Parameter efficiency:** The gains in parameter efficiency are substantial and well-documented.
    *   **Scalability to larger models:** Demonstrating results on a 32B parameter model provides concrete evidence of scalability.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited evaluation to multiple-choice tasks:** The paper primarily focuses on multiple-choice tasks, limiting the generalizability of the findings to other types of LLM applications (e.g., open-ended generation). The authors themselves acknowledge this in the limitations section.
    *   **Complexity of Implementation:**  While the idea is elegant, Stochastic Variational Inference combined with specific LoRA parameter handling adds to the implementation complexity compared to simpler methods. However, the method is built upon an existing framework.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While parameter efficient, the Bayesian model averaging step still adds a computational cost at inference time. More explicit analysis of this inference cost would be valuable.

* **Impact:**  This paper has the potential to influence future research by providing a practical and scalable method for uncertainty quantification in LLMs. It could lead to more widespread adoption of Bayesian methods in LLM applications, especially in high-stakes domains where reliable uncertainty estimates are crucial.

* **Score Justification:** ScalaBL presents a novel and significant advance by making Bayesian inference more practical for large language models through parameter-efficient subspace inference. Its demonstrable scalability, competitive performance, and parameter efficiency make it a valuable contribution. The main weakness is the limited diversity of downstream tasks used in the evaluation.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### XVerse: Consistent Multi-Subject Control of Identity and Semantic Attributes via DiT Modulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21416v1)
- **Authors**: Bowen Chen, Mengyi Zhao, Haomiao Sun, Li Chen, Xu Wang, Kang Du, Xinglong Wu
- **Abstract**: Achieving fine-grained control over subject identity and semantic attributes (pose, style, lighting) in text-to-image generation, particularly for multiple subjects, often undermines the editability and coherence of Diffusion Transformers (DiTs). Many approaches introduce artifacts or suffer from attribute entanglement. To overcome these challenges, we propose a novel multi-subject controlled generation model XVerse. By transforming reference images into offsets for token-specific text-stream modulation, XVerse allows for precise and independent control for specific subject without disrupting image latents or features. Consequently, XVerse offers high-fidelity, editable multi-subject image synthesis with robust control over individual subject characteristics and semantic attributes. This advancement significantly improves personalized and complex scene generation capabilities.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper and will provide a summary, a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, and a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper "XVerse: Consistent Multi-Subject Control of Identity and Semantic Attributes via DiT Modulation" introduces a novel framework, XVerse, for generating images with fine-grained control over subject identity and semantic attributes (pose, style, lighting) in multi-subject scenarios. The core idea is to leverage the modulation mechanism within Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) to inject information from reference images without disrupting the underlying image latents. XVerse transforms reference images into offsets for token-specific text-stream modulation, allowing precise and independent control over individual subjects and their attributes. Additionally, VAE-encoded image features are incorporated to enhance fine-grained details. The paper includes a newly constructed high-quality dataset, ablation studies, and a new benchmark (XVerseBench) to demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method in maintaining subject consistency, editability, and generating high-quality images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Approach:** The paper proposes a novel way to control multi-subject image generation by focusing on modulating the text-stream within DiTs, a technique that differs from directly manipulating attention mechanisms or injecting image features. This is a potentially more elegant and less disruptive approach.
    *   **Fine-Grained Control:**  The use of token-specific offsets and hierarchical modulation allows for precise control over individual subjects and their attributes, addressing the attribute entanglement issue common in other multi-subject generation methods.
    *   **High-Quality Results:** The qualitative and quantitative results on the newly created XVerseBench demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods in terms of subject consistency, editability, and overall image quality. The ablation studies provide evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed modules.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation, including a new benchmark specifically designed for multi-subject controllable generation. This adds value to the community by providing a standardized way to compare different methods. The new dataset will also be helpful.
    * **Clear writing:** The paper is well written and structured, making it relatively easy to understand the proposed method and its contributions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While the approach is novel in its specific implementation, it builds upon existing techniques in DiT modulation and attention mechanisms. The high level details of the architecture are not particularly inventive. The use of LoRA, FLUX and VAEs are all standard components. There is a sense that the core idea is relatively simple and the paper has been 'engineered' for performance on benchmark datasets.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Although the paper doesn't explicitly discuss computational cost, modulating the text-stream on a token specific basis seems like it would incur significant additional overhead. The increase is not quantified in the paper. It is also not mentioned if using this approach facilitates editing in image space through things like inversion.
    *   **Reliance on Large Datasets:** The method relies on a large, high-quality dataset for training. This could limit its accessibility and applicability in scenarios where such datasets are not available.
    *   **Limited Generalization Analysis:** While the paper demonstrates strong performance on XVerseBench, the generalization ability of the method to unseen subjects and scenarios is not thoroughly explored.  The impact of domain shift could be significant.
    *  **Ethical Discussion could be stronger:** The ethical discussion is broad and high level. Details on data filtering and construction are a little unclear.

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of this paper lies primarily in the specific combination of techniques—leveraging text-stream modulation with token-specific offsets and incorporating VAE features in a specific way. While the individual components are not entirely new, the way they are integrated to achieve fine-grained multi-subject control is a valuable contribution.
*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a relevant and challenging problem in image generation: achieving consistent and controllable multi-subject generation. The results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods, suggesting that the proposed approach has the potential to significantly advance the field. The introduction of XVerseBench provides a valuable tool for future research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses of the paper, I would assign it a score of **7**.  While the paper presents a novel and effective approach for multi-subject controllable generation, it builds upon existing techniques and has some limitations in terms of reliance on large datasets, computational cost, and generalization analysis. Also, while novel from an engineering perspective, the theoretical contributions are limited. However, the clear improvement over existing methods, the thorough evaluation, and the introduction of XVerseBench make it a significant contribution to the field, but the contributions are not on the level of a ground-breaking paper with a completely transformative idea.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Domain Knowledge-Enhanced LLMs for Fraud and Concept Drift Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21443v1)
- **Authors**: Ali Şenol, Garima Agrawal, Huan Liu
- **Abstract**: Detecting deceptive conversations on dynamic platforms is increasingly difficult due to evolving language patterns and Concept Drift (CD)-i.e., semantic or topical shifts that alter the context or intent of interactions over time. These shifts can obscure malicious intent or mimic normal dialogue, making accurate classification challenging. While Large Language Models (LLMs) show strong performance in natural language tasks, they often struggle with contextual ambiguity and hallucinations in risk-sensitive scenarios. To address these challenges, we present a Domain Knowledge (DK)-Enhanced LLM framework that integrates pretrained LLMs with structured, task-specific insights to perform fraud and concept drift detection. The proposed architecture consists of three main components: (1) a DK-LLM module to detect fake or deceptive conversations; (2) a drift detection unit (OCDD) to determine whether a semantic shift has occurred; and (3) a second DK-LLM module to classify the drift as either benign or fraudulent. We first validate the value of domain knowledge using a fake review dataset and then apply our full framework to SEConvo, a multiturn dialogue dataset that includes various types of fraud and spam attacks. Results show that our system detects fake conversations with high accuracy and effectively classifies the nature of drift. Guided by structured prompts, the LLaMA-based implementation achieves 98% classification accuracy. Comparative studies against zero-shot baselines demonstrate that incorporating domain knowledge and drift awareness significantly improves performance, interpretability, and robustness in high-stakes NLP applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of detecting fraudulent conversations and concept drift in dynamic online platforms. It proposes a domain-knowledge-enhanced, dual Large Language Model (LLM) framework. The system consists of three main components: a DK-LLM to detect fake conversations, an One-Class Concept Drift Detector (OCDD) to identify semantic shifts, and a second DK-LLM to classify the nature of the drift (benign or adversarial). The authors validate their framework on a fake review dataset and the SEConvo multi-turn dialogue dataset. Results indicate that incorporating domain knowledge and drift awareness significantly improves performance, interpretability, and robustness. The paper also benchmarks various LLMs (ChatGPT, Claude, LLaMA, DeepSeek) under zero-shot and domain-guided settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the combined approach of using domain knowledge with a dual-LLM architecture and statistical drift detection (OCDD) for a *unified* framework. This is more novel than just applying a single LLM to the task. Prior works have explored fraud detection and concept drift separately. The authors take a step toward integrating these tasks, which is commendable, but the individual components are not novel in themselves. The idea of using domain knowledge to improve LLM performance is also not brand new, however the application and detailed evaluation in this domain is new.
*   **Significance:** The significance is moderate. Fraud detection is a crucial problem, especially in online settings. Improving the accuracy, interpretability, and robustness of these systems is valuable. The focus on high-stakes NLP applications emphasizes the real-world importance of the work. The results suggest an improved performance in this field compared to traditional methods by addressing challenges like concept drifts and zero-shot performance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper demonstrates a performance improvement by incorporating domain knowledge into LLM prompting.
    *   The dual-LLM architecture combined with concept drift detection is a sound approach and facilitates more robust detection.
    *   The experimental results across multiple LLMs and datasets provide a strong foundation for the claims.
    *   The modular design enhances interpretability.
    *   Benchmarking several LLMs is valuable for the community.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty of individual components is limited. The OCDD approach and use of domain knowledge are not new, though their integration in this context is.
    *   The reliance on structured prompts might limit the generalizability of the approach to different types of conversations or platforms. A more adaptive or learned approach might be preferable.
    *   The paper does not address the computational costs of the dual-LLM framework adequately, especially since LLMs are resource-intensive. While mentioned in the discussion, a more detailed analysis would be helpful.
    *   The paper does not extensively delve into the qualitative analysis of errors or failure cases. It would be beneficial to understand the types of fraudulent conversations the system still struggles with.
    *   The performance gain with ChatGPT was marginal, which raises some questions about the prompt design or the model's sensitivity to domain knowledge in this specific task.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a sound and potentially impactful framework for fraud and concept drift detection. The integration of domain knowledge with a dual-LLM architecture and drift detection is a valuable contribution, and the results demonstrate a noticeable improvement over baseline methods, particularly with the LLaMA models. However, the limited novelty of individual components, reliance on structured prompts, and lack of detailed computational analysis warrant a slightly lower score. The paper offers a meaningful step forward, but more substantial innovations are needed to significantly advance the field. The paper provides important insights into how to improve LLM performance in high-stakes applications through domain knowledge and by addressing concept drift. However the challenges of computational costs and generalizability limits the applicability of the framework.
Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Text2Cypher Across Languages: Evaluating Foundational Models Beyond English
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21445v1)
- **Authors**: Makbule Gulcin Ozsoy, William Tai
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models have enabled natural language interfaces that translate user questions into database queries, such as Text2SQL, Text2SPARQL, and Text2Cypher. While these interfaces enhance database accessibility, most research today focuses solely on English, with limited evaluation in other languages. This paper investigates the performance of foundational LLMs on the Text2Cypher task across multiple languages. We create and release a multilingual test set by translating English questions into Spanish and Turkish while preserving the original Cypher queries, enabling fair cross-lingual comparison. We evaluate multiple foundational models using standardized prompts and metrics. Our results show a consistent performance pattern: highest on English, then Spanish, and lowest on Turkish. We attribute this to differences in training data availability and linguistic characteristics. Additionally, we explore the impact of translating task prompts into Spanish and Turkish. Results show little to no change in evaluation metrics, suggesting prompt translation has minor impact. Our findings highlight the need for more inclusive evaluation and development in multilingual query generation. Future work includes schema localization and fine-tuning across diverse languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Text2Cypher Across Languages: Evaluating Foundational Models Beyond English" investigates the performance of foundational Large Language Models (LLMs) on the Text2Cypher task in English, Spanish, and Turkish.  The authors create and release a multilingual test set by translating English questions into Spanish and Turkish, ensuring semantic alignment and fair cross-lingual comparison. They evaluate several foundational LLMs using standardized prompts and metrics (ROUGE-L and execution-based Exact Match). The results indicate that models consistently perform best on English, followed by Spanish, and worst on Turkish. The authors attribute this performance pattern to differences in training data availability and linguistic characteristics (agglutinative nature of Turkish). Furthermore, they explore the impact of translating task prompts into Spanish and Turkish and find that prompt translation has minimal impact on the execution-based evaluation metric. The paper highlights the need for more inclusive evaluation and development in multilingual query generation, suggesting future work including schema localization and fine-tuning across diverse languages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in its explicit and focused effort to evaluate foundational LLMs on the Text2Cypher task *across multiple languages* beyond the typical English-centric focus. While multilingual evaluations exist for Text2SQL, the specific application to Text2Cypher and the release of a specifically designed multilingual test set is a valuable contribution. The exploration of prompt translation impact is also interesting, though the findings are somewhat negative (showing minimal impact).

**Significance:**  The paper's significance is moderate. It highlights the limitations of current foundational LLMs when applied to non-English languages in the specific domain of graph database query generation. The findings underscore the importance of considering linguistic diversity and resource availability when developing and evaluating LLMs for practical applications. The released dataset provides a useful benchmark for future research in this area.  However, the results themselves are not entirely surprising, confirming well-established observations about LLM performance across languages.

**Strengths:**

*   **Multilingual Dataset:** The creation and release of a semantically aligned multilingual Text2Cypher test set is a significant contribution, enabling more fair and comprehensive evaluation. The dataset addresses a gap in existing resources.
*   **Clear Experiment Design:** The experimental setup is well-defined, using standardized prompts and established evaluation metrics (ROUGE-L and Exact Match). This ensures reproducibility and comparability.
*   **Focused Problem:** The paper tackles a specific problem (Text2Cypher) within the broader context of multilingual LLMs, allowing for a more in-depth analysis.
*   **Practical Implications:** The findings highlight the need for more inclusive development and evaluation practices in multilingual query generation, which has practical implications for database accessibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to only three languages (English, Spanish, and Turkish). While representing varying resource levels and language families, a broader range of languages would strengthen the findings.
*   **Modest Results:** While the negative impact of prompt translation is an interesting finding, the general performance pattern (English > Spanish > Turkish) is not particularly surprising, given existing knowledge about LLM performance across languages.  The analysis, while adequate, could be more detailed in examining error types and patterns for each language.
*   **Missing Error Analysis:** The paper does not include a detailed qualitative error analysis. What kinds of errors are made in Spanish and Turkish? Are these errors related to syntax, semantics, or a combination thereof? A qualitative error analysis would greatly enhance the contribution and suggest pathways for future research.
*   **Shallow exploration of schema localization:** While mentioned as future work, the paper gives no actual result or hypothesis related to schema localization, and is purely a promise.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6**.

*Reasoning:* While the paper's explicit focus on multilingual Text2Cypher evaluation and the release of a specific dataset are valuable contributions (justifying a score above the average), the findings themselves are somewhat incremental and the limitations discussed above prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking or highly impactful work. The limitations outweigh the novelty and the lack of deeper analysis brings down the value. There is a value contribution, but the lack of surprises leaves a bit to be desired.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Controllable 3D Placement of Objects with Scene-Aware Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21446v1)
- **Authors**: Mohamed Omran, Dimitris Kalatzis, Jens Petersen, Amirhossein Habibian, Auke Wiggers
- **Abstract**: Image editing approaches have become more powerful and flexible with the advent of powerful text-conditioned generative models. However, placing objects in an environment with a precise location and orientation still remains a challenge, as this typically requires carefully crafted inpainting masks or prompts. In this work, we show that a carefully designed visual map, combined with coarse object masks, is sufficient for high quality object placement. We design a conditioning signal that resolves ambiguities, while being flexible enough to allow for changing of shapes or object orientations. By building on an inpainting model, we leave the background intact by design, in contrast to methods that model objects and background jointly. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our method in the automotive setting, where we compare different conditioning signals in novel object placement tasks. These tasks are designed to measure edit quality not only in terms of appearance, but also in terms of pose and location accuracy, including cases that require non-trivial shape changes. Lastly, we show that fine location control can be combined with appearance control to place existing objects in precise locations in a scene.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the problem of precisely placing 3D objects in images using diffusion models. The authors propose a novel conditioning signal based on projecting a 3D bounding box onto the image plane. This visual map, combined with coarse object masks, is used as input to a diffusion-based inpainting model. This approach allows for control over object location and orientation while preserving the background. The method is evaluated in an automotive setting, demonstrating its effectiveness in terms of pose fidelity, realism, and the ability to replace existing objects, even with shape changes. The authors also show that the method can be combined with a visual encoder to place specific objects in precise locations, controlling both appearance and pose.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using a projected 3D bounding box as a visual conditioning signal is a reasonable and novel contribution. While other works have explored coordinate tokens or depth maps, the authors argue that their approach offers a better balance of control, flexibility, and ease of implementation. The specific design of the visual map, with two triangles per face and a wireframe, adds another layer of novelty by resolving ambiguities in object orientation. Combining this visual signal with a standard inpainting model, leveraging existing pre-trained models while only fine-tuning relevant parts, is also a strength.
*   **Significance:** The problem of precise object placement is important in various domains, especially for generating synthetic data for training robust machine learning models. The automotive domain is a relevant and well-motivated use case. The paper's success in achieving high pose fidelity and realism could have a significant impact on applications such as autonomous driving simulation and augmented reality. The ability to control both object appearance and location further enhances the method's practical value.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem definition and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of existing approaches and motivates the need for a more controllable and flexible method.
    *   **Well-designed conditioning signal:** The proposed visual map is intuitive and effective in encoding 3D location and pose information.
    *   **Practical implementation:** The method builds upon existing techniques and pre-trained models, making it relatively easy to implement and apply.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough evaluation with multiple baselines, metrics, and ablation studies, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Qualitative results:** The qualitative results showcase the method's ability to handle complex scenes and object transformations.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited scale:** While the authors argue that generating objects one-by-one is not a limitation, it does make the method less computationally efficient than approaches that render full scenes at once. It would be important to validate this in comparison to other scene level generators (with more complicated scene).
    *   **Dependency on 3D annotations:** The method relies on accurate 3D bounding box annotations for training, which may not be available in all domains. The reliance on existing 3D detection might limit the method to the fidelity of the detector being used.
    *   **Ablation study of the encoding:** Though they ablate different ways of encoding with mesh or wireframes, it is not clear how important it is to encode with specific colors.
    *   **Limited discussion of failure cases:** The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the limitations of the method and potential failure cases, such as occlusions or complex object interactions.
    *   **Minor improvements to the standard stable diffusion fine-tuning regime:** While the proposed method is innovative in utilizing bounding boxes to condition the image generation, the improvements to the already well-established field of diffusion models for in-painting seems only incremental.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work provides a solid foundation for future research in controllable object placement and scene generation. It could inspire new techniques for encoding 3D information in visual conditioning signals and for leveraging diffusion models for image editing tasks. The method's practical value could lead to its adoption in various applications, particularly in the automotive domain.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and effective approach for precisely placing 3D objects in images using diffusion models. The proposed visual conditioning signal is well-designed and the method is thoroughly evaluated. The results demonstrate the method's ability to achieve high pose fidelity and realism while preserving the background. The ability to combine appearance and pose control further enhances the method's practical value. However, the paper is limited by its reliance on 3D annotations and its incremental improvements in the already well-established field of diffusion models. More discussion of the limitations and failure cases would further strengthen the contribution. Overall, the paper represents a significant contribution to the field and has the potential to influence future research and applications. The improvements to the standard stable diffusion fine-tuning regime are good but only incremental in the in-painting world. The improvements over current object aware diffusion models is the larger contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ThinkSound: Chain-of-Thought Reasoning in Multimodal Large Language Models for Audio Generation and Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21448v1)
- **Authors**: Huadai Liu, Jialei Wang, Kaicheng Luo, Wen Wang, Qian Chen, Zhou Zhao, Wei Xue
- **Abstract**: While end-to-end video-to-audio generation has greatly improved, producing high-fidelity audio that authentically captures the nuances of visual content remains challenging. Like professionals in the creative industries, such generation requires sophisticated reasoning about items such as visual dynamics, acoustic environments, and temporal relationships. We present \textbf{ThinkSound}, a novel framework that leverages Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning to enable stepwise, interactive audio generation and editing for videos. Our approach decomposes the process into three complementary stages: foundational foley generation that creates semantically coherent soundscapes, interactive object-centric refinement through precise user interactions, and targeted editing guided by natural language instructions. At each stage, a multimodal large language model generates contextually aligned CoT reasoning that guides a unified audio foundation model. Furthermore, we introduce \textbf{AudioCoT}, a comprehensive dataset with structured reasoning annotations that establishes connections between visual content, textual descriptions, and sound synthesis. Experiments demonstrate that ThinkSound achieves state-of-the-art performance in video-to-audio generation across both audio metrics and CoT metrics and excels in out-of-distribution Movie Gen Audio benchmark. The demo page is available at https://ThinkSound-Demo.github.io.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the ThinkSound paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ThinkSound, a novel framework for video-to-audio (V2A) generation that leverages Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The approach decomposes audio generation into three user-centric stages: foundational foley generation, interactive object-centric refinement (through user clicks), and targeted audio editing (using natural language instructions). At each stage, an MLLM generates contextually aligned CoT reasoning to guide a unified audio foundation model. The paper also presents AudioCoT, a large-scale dataset with structured reasoning annotations that connect visual content, text descriptions, and sound synthesis. Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance in V2A generation, according to audio metrics and CoT metrics, especially on out-of-distribution data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper possesses significant novelty on several fronts:

    *   **CoT for V2A:**  The use of CoT reasoning to guide audio generation in a V2A setting is a significant departure from previous end-to-end approaches. It explicitly addresses the need for structured reasoning about visual dynamics, acoustic environments, and temporal relationships, mimicking the workflow of sound designers.
    *   **Interactive Refinement:** The interactive refinement stage, guided by user clicks, is another novel aspect. It allows users to directly influence the audio generation process based on specific visual elements, enabling a level of control not seen in previous works.
    *   **Unified Framework:** The unified foundation model, trained with CoT instructions, is a key contribution.  It streamlines the audio generation pipeline, avoiding the fragmentation of previous MLLM-based approaches.
    *   **AudioCoT Dataset:** The creation and public release of AudioCoT fill a critical gap in the availability of datasets with structured reasoning annotations for V2A tasks. This will likely stimulate further research in the area.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field due to:

    *   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on standard V2A benchmarks and out-of-distribution datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of the CoT-guided approach.
    *   **Increased User Control:** The interactive and editing capabilities enhance the usability and practical relevance of V2A systems. Sound designers and other creative professionals can leverage ThinkSound to produce high-fidelity audio with fine-grained control.
    *   **Research Inspiration:** The introduction of the AudioCoT dataset is likely to facilitate future research in CoT-based audio generation, multimodal reasoning, and interactive audio editing.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined and intuitive framework.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation using both objective and subjective metrics.
    *   Ablation studies to demonstrate the importance of different components.
    *   Release of a valuable dataset (AudioCoT).
    *   Demo page showing the model's capabilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on GPT-4.1-nano for CoT generation is a potential limitation, as it may not be easily accessible or scalable for all researchers.
    *   The reliance on proprietary models and the architecture complexity can hinder reproducibility by the broader research community.
    *   While the paper shows improvements, the qualitative examples might benefit from more detailed comparisons to baselines, showing the exact improvements of the method.
    *   Ethical considerations are well addressed, but further discussion on potential societal impacts and misuse scenarios could be included.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by:

    *   Shifting the focus from end-to-end approaches to more structured and interpretable reasoning methods for V2A generation.
    *   Promoting the development of interactive audio generation systems that empower users with greater creative control.
    *   Encouraging the creation of more comprehensive and annotated datasets for multimodal reasoning and audio synthesis.
    *   Providing a strong baseline and framework for future research in CoT-guided audio generation.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** While the paper presents solid results and novel methodology, it has a dependency on proprietary models and datasets, potentially limiting broader adoption. The significance of ThinkSound is in its innovative architecture and demonstrable performance improvements, establishing a new standard for V2A generation that incorporates interactive elements.

Score: 8

**Rigorous Rationale:** ThinkSound demonstrates a significant advancement in V2A generation through its innovative incorporation of CoT reasoning and user interactivity. The rigorous evaluation validates the effectiveness of the approach, and the introduction of the AudioCoT dataset significantly contributes to the research community. While it has certain reliance on proprietary models and datasets, and architectural complexity limiting widespread reproducibility, its influence on the evolution of audio synthesis systems and the interactive control given to users is likely to be substantial, justifying a high score. The weaknesses prevent it from being in the 9-10 range which represents truly exceptional and transformative work but is a significant contribution to the area.

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 8/10

### Rethinking Oversaturation in Classifier-Free Guidance via Low Frequency
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21452v1)
- **Authors**: Kaiyu Song, Hanjiang Lai
- **Abstract**: Classifier-free guidance (CFG) succeeds in condition diffusion models that use a guidance scale to balance the influence of conditional and unconditional terms. A high guidance scale is used to enhance the performance of the conditional term. However, the high guidance scale often results in oversaturation and unrealistic artifacts. In this paper, we introduce a new perspective based on low-frequency signals, identifying the accumulation of redundant information in these signals as the key factor behind oversaturation and unrealistic artifacts. Building on this insight, we propose low-frequency improved classifier-free guidance (LF-CFG) to mitigate these issues. Specifically, we introduce an adaptive threshold-based measurement to pinpoint the locations of redundant information. We determine a reasonable threshold by analyzing the change rate of low-frequency information between prior and current steps. We then apply a down-weight strategy to reduce the impact of redundant information in the low-frequency signals. Experimental results demonstrate that LF-CFG effectively alleviates oversaturation and unrealistic artifacts across various diffusion models, including Stable Diffusion-XL, Stable Diffusion 2.1, 3.0, 3.5, and SiT-XL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Rethinking Oversaturation in Classifier-Free Guidance via Low Frequency" addresses the problem of oversaturation artifacts that occur in classifier-free guidance (CFG) when using high guidance scales in diffusion models. The authors propose a novel perspective based on low-frequency signals, arguing that the accumulation of redundant information in these signals is the root cause of the problem. They introduce Low-Frequency improved Classifier-Free Guidance (LF-CFG), which uses an adaptive threshold-based measurement to identify locations of redundant information in the low-frequency signals and applies a down-weighting strategy to mitigate their impact. Experimental results on various diffusion models demonstrate that LF-CFG effectively alleviates oversaturation and improves image quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively novel perspective on the oversaturation problem in CFG. While previous work has focused on adjusting the guidance scale or reformulating the guidance mechanism itself (APG), this paper delves into the frequency domain, identifying low-frequency signals as a critical factor. This frequency-based analysis is a contribution. The adaptive thresholding based on rate of change is also a unique approach for identifying redundant regions, and downweighting based on identified regions is well reasoned.

*   **Significance:** The oversaturation problem is a known limitation of CFG, hindering the full potential of diffusion models. Addressing this issue is important for improving the quality and realism of generated images. LF-CFG demonstrates promising results in mitigating oversaturation across multiple diffusion models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Intuitive Explanation:** The paper provides a clear and intuitive explanation of why low-frequency signals contribute to oversaturation.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The proposed LF-CFG method is well-defined and technically sound.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of LF-CFG in alleviating oversaturation and improving image quality on multiple diffusion models.
    *   **Broad Applicability:** The method is shown to be compatible with various diffusion model architectures and solver.
    *   **Computation efficiency:** Computation efficiency is preserved.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited theoretical justification for the threshold.** The chosen method mean + std while effective may not be mathematically optimal. The method uses Gaussian distribution as a baseline, though not all signals might conform.
    *   **Over-Generality:** While tested on several Stable Diffusion variants, its performance on other, structurally different, diffusion architectures, or for other modalities (e.g., audio, video), is unknown. The method is based on rate of change, implying that non stationary signals will be less performant. This is not discussed in the paper.
    *   **Limited analysis of failure cases:** The paper does not specifically delve into cases where the proposed LF-CFG might fail or produce undesirable artifacts. More in-depth error analysis would strengthen the work.

*   **Potential Influence:** The frequency-based perspective on oversaturation could influence future research in this area. LF-CFG provides a practical solution for mitigating oversaturation, which could be adopted in various applications of diffusion models. The approach is also complementary to other improvement methods (PAG).

*The paper lacks critical analysis of the downsides of LF-CFG*.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the problem, proposed solution, and experimental results. The figures are well-designed and support the claims made in the paper.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper makes a solid contribution to the understanding and mitigation of oversaturation in classifier-free guidance for diffusion models. Its novelty lies in the frequency domain analysis of the problem, leading to a straightforward and effective solution. The empirical validation is convincing, and the method is applicable to multiple diffusion models. While the method is not universal, and theoretical justification has its flaws, the paper offers a fresh perspective and practical benefits. It is a significant improvement over baseline architectures that will have noticeable implications in terms of training regimes.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SmoothSinger: A Conditional Diffusion Model for Singing Voice Synthesis with Multi-Resolution Architecture
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21478v1)
- **Authors**: Kehan Sui, Jinxu Xiang, Fang Jin
- **Abstract**: Singing voice synthesis (SVS) aims to generate expressive and high-quality vocals from musical scores, requiring precise modeling of pitch, duration, and articulation. While diffusion-based models have achieved remarkable success in image and video generation, their application to SVS remains challenging due to the complex acoustic and musical characteristics of singing, often resulting in artifacts that degrade naturalness. In this work, we propose SmoothSinger, a conditional diffusion model designed to synthesize high quality and natural singing voices. Unlike prior methods that depend on vocoders as a final stage and often introduce distortion, SmoothSinger refines low-quality synthesized audio directly in a unified framework, mitigating the degradation associated with two-stage pipelines. The model adopts a reference-guided dual-branch architecture, using low-quality audio from any baseline system as a reference to guide the denoising process, enabling more expressive and context-aware synthesis. Furthermore, it enhances the conventional U-Net with a parallel low-frequency upsampling path, allowing the model to better capture pitch contours and long term spectral dependencies. To improve alignment during training, we replace reference audio with degraded ground truth audio, addressing temporal mismatch between reference and target signals. Experiments on the Opencpop dataset, a large-scale Chinese singing corpus, demonstrate that SmoothSinger achieves state-of-the-art results in both objective and subjective evaluations. Extensive ablation studies confirm its effectiveness in reducing artifacts and improving the naturalness of synthesized voices.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SmoothSinger, a novel conditional diffusion model for high-fidelity singing voice synthesis (SVS). It addresses limitations in existing SVS systems, particularly those arising from the two-stage acoustic model-vocoder pipeline. SmoothSinger employs a reference-guided dual-branch architecture, incorporating low-quality audio as guidance during the denoising process, and introduces a multi-resolution (MR) module that enhances the conventional U-Net with a parallel low-frequency upsampling path to better capture pitch contours and spectral dependencies. A training strategy involving degraded ground truth audio is also used to improve alignment. Experimental results on the Opencpop dataset show that SmoothSinger achieves state-of-the-art performance in terms of both objective and subjective evaluations. The authors further demonstrate the model's adaptability by applying it to text-to-speech (TTS) synthesis on the LJSpeech dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a combination of several techniques, which, while not entirely novel individually, are integrated in a unique way within the context of SVS. The key novel aspects include:

*   **Reference-guided dual-branch architecture *combined* with a diffusion model for SVS:** Using a reference audio signal to guide a diffusion process is not entirely new, but its implementation within a SVS context, *specifically* combined with the architecture proposed is a significant contribution. This promotes context-awareness and potentially captures the nuanced musical details from a reference.
*   **Multi-resolution (MR) module for low-frequency enhancement:**  While multi-resolution techniques are well-established in audio processing, *specifically* tailoring this to improve pitch and low frequency components of the audio is useful for SVS, which often suffers from artifacts in these areas. The design of this module for parallel processing, is also unique.
*   **Degraded ground truth audio for training:** This addresses a practical issue of temporal misalignment in diffusion models, which is specific to the SVS setup and a clever way to stabilize training.
*  **Unified framework avoiding vocoders:** Combining all of these together into an unified diffusion framework is significant, since it helps mitigate issues of two-stage vocoder models.

**Significance:**

*   **Improved Audio Quality:** The paper demonstrably shows improvements in audio quality through both objective and subjective metrics. This is a significant advancement because high-fidelity and naturalness are primary goals of SVS research.
*   **Robustness and Generalizability:**  The paper not only shows performance on the Opencpop dataset but also demonstrates its adaptability to TTS on LJSpeech.
*   **Potential Impact on the Field:**  The work is well-executed, and shows promising results. It serves as a valuable baseline for future research on diffusion models in SVS.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper uses a diverse set of evaluation metrics (MOS, Ranking, SIG MOS, BAK MOS, PESQ, STOI) which provides more reliability.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies rigorously demonstrate the contribution of each key component.
*   **Clear Writing and Presentation:** The paper is well-written, and the model architecture is clearly illustrated.
*   **Addresses a real problem:** Vocoder issues are known problems in SVS models.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is novel, some of the individual components are based on existing research. This slightly diminishes the overall novelty.
*   **Limited Scope of Datasets:** While Opencpop is a useful dataset, it only contains a single singer. It might not fully generalize to other singers.
*   **Computational Cost:** The paper does not explicitly address the computational cost of the model, which could be a limiting factor for real-time applications.  Diffusion models are generally more expensive than simpler generative methods.
*   **Potential Overfitting to Opencpop:** One can suggest that there may be some overfitting involved to this dataset, especially since there's only one singer.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the above analysis, a score of 7.5 seems appropriate. The paper has a *reasonable* amount of novelty due to the combination of existing techniques in a specific SVS context, and achieves demonstrably better results than existing approaches. This provides tangible benefits to the field. The ablation studies also strengthen the conclusions. However, the limitations regarding computational cost, and single speaker overfitting mentioned above prevent it from achieving a higher score. It's a well-executed research project with practical contributions, but not necessarily groundbreaking.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Bridging Offline and Online Reinforcement Learning for LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21495v1)
- **Authors**: Jack Lanchantin, Angelica Chen, Janice Lan, Xian Li, Swarnadeep Saha, Tianlu Wang, Jing Xu, Ping Yu, Weizhe Yuan, Jason E Weston, Sainbayar Sukhbaatar, Ilia Kulikov
- **Abstract**: We investigate the effectiveness of reinforcement learning methods for finetuning large language models when transitioning from offline to semi-online to fully online regimes for both verifiable and non-verifiable tasks. Our experiments cover training on verifiable math as well as non-verifiable instruction following with a set of benchmark evaluations for both. Across these settings, we extensively compare online and semi-online Direct Preference Optimization and Group Reward Policy Optimization objectives, and surprisingly find similar performance and convergence between these variants, which all strongly outperform offline methods. We provide a detailed analysis of the training dynamics and hyperparameter selection strategies to achieve optimal results. Finally, we show that multi-tasking with verifiable and non-verifiable rewards jointly yields improved performance across both task types.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of reinforcement learning (RL) methods for fine-tuning large language models (LLMs), focusing on the transition from offline to semi-online to fully online training regimes. It compares Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) and Group Reward Policy Optimization (GRPO) across both verifiable (math) and non-verifiable (instruction following) tasks. The key findings include that semi-online and fully online training methods significantly outperform offline DPO.  Furthermore, semi-online DPO achieves performance comparable to online DPO and GRPO with potential efficiency gains. The paper also explores joint optimization of verifiable and non-verifiable rewards, showing improvements across both task types. The authors provide an analysis of training dynamics and hyperparameter selection strategies to achieve optimal results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The systematic comparison of offline, semi-online, and online training paradigms for LLM fine-tuning across both verifiable and non-verifiable tasks is a valuable contribution. While DPO and GRPO are existing techniques, their relative performance and the bridging of offline to online training regimes have not been explored extensively. The finding that semi-online DPO can achieve comparable performance to online methods with potential efficiency gains presents a novel trade-off. The investigation of joint training with both reward types and its implications for generalization is also a valuable addition.

*   **Significance:** Understanding the relative effectiveness of different training paradigms is crucial for efficient LLM alignment. The paper sheds light on the limitations of purely offline DPO and demonstrates the importance of training with updated model responses. The potential efficiency of semi-online DPO has practical implications for scaling RL-based fine-tuning. The successful combination of verifiable and non-verifiable rewards demonstrates a path toward more general and robust LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Systematic comparison of different training regimes (offline, semi-online, online).
    *   Evaluation on both verifiable and non-verifiable tasks.
    *   Detailed analysis of training dynamics and hyperparameter selection.
    *   Exploration of joint optimization of different reward types.
    *   Clear and well-structured presentation of results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments are limited to one LLM model (Llama-3.1-8B). The results might not generalize to other models.
    *   Some of the observations, such as the length bias in reward models, are already known.
    *   Although the paper explores entropy regularization and multi-objective loss, it would benefit from more robust ablations for better understanding the efficacy of those methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence the field by encouraging researchers to move beyond purely offline DPO and consider semi-online or online alternatives. The finding about the potential efficiency gains of semi-online DPO could drive further investigation into this training paradigm. The results of joint reward optimization could motivate more research into multi-task LLM alignment strategies.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a valuable systematic analysis with several novel findings, but has limitations. The focus of evaluation with only one LLM architecture can be perceived as limiting. Overall the paper provides important insights into LLM fine-tuning and helps guide future research in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### "What's Up, Doc?": Analyzing How Users Seek Health Information in Large-Scale Conversational AI Datasets
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21532v1)
- **Authors**: Akshay Paruchuri, Maryam Aziz, Rohit Vartak, Ayman Ali, Best Uchehara, Xin Liu, Ishan Chatterjee, Monica Agrawal
- **Abstract**: People are increasingly seeking healthcare information from large language models (LLMs) via interactive chatbots, yet the nature and inherent risks of these conversations remain largely unexplored. In this paper, we filter large-scale conversational AI datasets to achieve HealthChat-11K, a curated dataset of 11K real-world conversations composed of 25K user messages. We use HealthChat-11K and a clinician-driven taxonomy for how users interact with LLMs when seeking healthcare information in order to systematically study user interactions across 21 distinct health specialties. Our analysis reveals insights into the nature of how and why users seek health information, such as common interactions, instances of incomplete context, affective behaviors, and interactions (e.g., leading questions) that can induce sycophancy, underscoring the need for improvements in the healthcare support capabilities of LLMs deployed as conversational AI. Code and artifacts to retrieve our analyses and combine them into a curated dataset can be found here: https://github.com/yahskapar/HealthChat
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "What's Up, Doc?": Analyzing How Users Seek Health Information in Large-Scale Conversational AI Datasets."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the growing trend of individuals seeking healthcare information from LLM-based chatbots, highlighting the lack of research focusing on how users *actually* interact with these systems in real-world scenarios.  To bridge this gap, the authors create HealthChat-11K, a curated dataset of 11K real-world conversations extracted from larger conversational AI datasets (LMSYS-Chat-1M and WildChat-1M). They develop and apply a clinician-driven taxonomy to categorize user interactions across 21 health specialties, enabling a systematic analysis of how and why people seek health information via chatbots.  The analysis reveals common interaction patterns, instances of incomplete context, affective behaviors, and potentially sycophancy-inducing leading questions, underscoring the need for improved healthcare capabilities in conversational LLMs. The code and curated dataset are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty of the paper lies in the creation and analysis of the HealthChat-11K dataset using a clinician-informed taxonomy. While other datasets and studies touch upon the use of chatbots for health information, this work distinguishes itself by:

    *   Focusing on real-world conversations rather than synthetic data or single-turn queries.
    *   Developing a specific taxonomy grounded in clinical expertise to categorize user interactions.
    *   Systematically analyzing user behaviors to identify patterns and potential risks.
    *   The work focuses on a user-centric analysis.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to inform the development of safer and more effective healthcare chatbots. By revealing how users interact with these systems, the study identifies key areas for improvement, such as:

    *   Handling incomplete or ambiguous user context.
    *   Mitigating biases like sycophancy in model responses.
    *   Addressing affective expressions in user queries.
    *   Improving LLMs' ability to provide appropriate information and care navigation

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper has a clear and well-defined research question.
    *   The creation of HealthChat-11K is a valuable contribution, providing a much-needed resource for the field.
    *   The clinician-driven taxonomy adds rigor and clinical relevance to the analysis.
    *   The analysis is systematic and identifies actionable insights.
    *   Releasing the dataset and code promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dataset curation process, while described, relies on LLM-based filtering which, while showing reasonable human-LLM concordance, introduces some inherent imperfections (as acknowledged in the limitations section). This means that noise in the dataset must be considered.
    *   The analysis primarily focuses on user behavior and does not delve deeply into the quality of the model responses. While beyond the scope of this paper, this dyadic aspect should be considered for future work.
    *   The case studies, while illustrative, are limited in scope and depth.
    *   The analysis is limited to English-language conversations, which limits the generalizability of the findings. The use of specific LLMs adds a bias to the overall work.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of future healthcare chatbots by:

    *   Providing a framework for analyzing user interactions and identifying potential risks.
    *   Guiding the design of more robust and user-centered dialogue management strategies.
    *   Informing the development of new benchmarks and evaluation metrics for healthcare chatbots.
    *   Increasing awareness of the limitations and potential dangers of using LLMs for healthcare advice.
    *   The paper also opens the avenue for better model training that is targeted at handling issues like leading questions or addressing incomplete context.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a valuable dataset and analysis, it's essential to consider its limitations. The LLM filtering processes, exclusion of model responses, and limited scope of the case studies prevent it from being a truly exceptional contribution.

    The core contribution, the HealthChat-11K dataset, is indeed novel in its construction and intended use. The taxonomy provides a structured approach to understanding user interactions. However, the dependence on other datasets (LMSYS-Chat-1M and WildChat-1M) means the work is derivative, and those base datasets bring with them their own biases. The study would have been strengthened by a more in-depth evaluation of the LLM responses.

    The findings will benefit the development of more advanced AI-based healthcare solutions. However, more comprehensive longitudinal analyses are needed.

**Score: 7**

Rationale:
The paper is a valuable and novel contribution to the field, providing a much-needed resource and framework for analyzing real-world user interactions with healthcare chatbots. Its systematic analysis and actionable insights have the potential to influence the development of safer and more effective systems. However, inherent limitations in dataset curation, the focus on user behavior only, case study depth, and the use of only English limits its overall impact and generalizability.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Exploring the Design Space of 3D MLLMs for CT Report Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21535v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammed Baharoon, Jun Ma, Congyu Fang, Augustin Toma, Bo Wang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have emerged as a promising way to automate Radiology Report Generation (RRG). In this work, we systematically investigate the design space of 3D MLLMs, including visual input representation, projectors, Large Language Models (LLMs), and fine-tuning techniques for 3D CT report generation. We also introduce two knowledge-based report augmentation methods that improve performance on the GREEN score by up to 10\%, achieving the 2nd place on the MICCAI 2024 AMOS-MM challenge. Our results on the 1,687 cases from the AMOS-MM dataset show that RRG is largely independent of the size of LLM under the same training protocol. We also show that larger volume size does not always improve performance if the original ViT was pre-trained on a smaller volume size. Lastly, we show that using a segmentation mask along with the CT volume improves performance. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/bowang-lab/AMOS-MM-Solution
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the design space of 3D Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) for automating radiology report generation (RRG) from CT scans. The authors systematically investigate different architectural choices, including visual input representations, projectors, Large Language Models (LLMs), and fine-tuning techniques.  They also introduce two knowledge-based report augmentation methods – Binary-based Questioning (BQ) and Naive Normality (NN) – to improve report completeness. They evaluate their approach on the AMOS-MM dataset, achieving second place in the MICCAI 2024 AMOS-MM challenge. The key findings suggest that RRG performance is largely independent of LLM size, and that careful consideration of pre-training data volume size for the ViT is crucial. Additionally, using segmentation masks alongside CT volumes improves performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Systematic Exploration:** The paper conducts a comprehensive exploration of the design space of 3D MLLMs for RRG. This systematic approach provides valuable insights into the impact of different architectural choices.
    * **Knowledge-based Augmentation:** The introduction of BQ and NN augmentation methods is a strong point. The experimental results demonstrate a significant improvement in the GREEN score, indicating improved report completeness.  The qualitative examples effectively illustrate how these methods capture missed positive findings and ensure completeness.
    * **Strong Evaluation and Results:** The paper presents results on a challenging dataset (AMOS-MM) and achieves strong performance (second place in the challenge). The use of multiple evaluation metrics (GREEN, RaTEScore, BLEU, ROUGE, METEOR) provides a more comprehensive assessment of the generated reports.
    * **Open-source code:** Providing open-source code enables reproducibility and facilitates further research in the area.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **LLM Independence Claim:** While the paper claims that RRG performance is largely LLM-independent, this conclusion might be limited by the specific LLMs tested and the training protocol used. Further investigation with a wider range of LLMs and training strategies is warranted. A more cautious phrasing here would be beneficial.
    * **Impact of Input resolution:** The negative impact of increasing image resolution, despite using AnyResolution, is a surprising result that needs further explanation. The authors speculate about the mismatch between pre-training and fine-tuning resolutions, but a deeper analysis would strengthen the paper. More details about implementation and hyperparameter tuning for AnyRes are needed to further understand this result.
    * **Limited Novelty of Individual Components:** While the overall system is effective, many of the components (e.g., the ViT architecture, AnyResolution technique, LLaVA framework) are not novel contributions of this paper. The novelty lies primarily in the systematic exploration and application of these existing techniques to the 3D RRG task and the proposed report augmentation methods.
    * **Naive Normality concerns** While the technique shows promising results, the authors mention the reduced text similarity scores after its application, which may indicate a possible trade-off between completeness and accuracy/originality.
    * **Use of GPT3.5-mini** Relying on GPT 3.5 mini to convert reports into triplets introduces a potential bias, since the performance can be influenced by the ability of the tool to extract information from the source.

* **Significance:**

    * The paper addresses an important problem: automating radiology report generation to alleviate the workload of radiologists.
    * The systematic exploration of the design space and the introduction of knowledge-based augmentation methods are valuable contributions to the field.
    * The strong experimental results demonstrate the potential of 3D MLLMs for RRG and provide a solid foundation for future research.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of medical image analysis and natural language processing. The systematic approach, the introduction of the report augmentation methods, and the strong experimental results are all commendable. However, the reliance on existing architectures and the surprising results regarding input resolution and text similarity metrics detract from the overall novelty and impact.  Therefore, a score of 7 is justified.
**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10
